```text
Within 120 days after the effective date of this Code the Administrator shall make a report to the President showing the effect of the Code, and particularly the effect of the provisions of Article V thereof, during the period of 90 days following its effective date, in such detail as to permit the President to determine within said 120 days whether, in order to effectuate the policy of the Act, any of the provisions of the Code should be modified or eliminated.
```

### T47e448dbb872a60cfbee08c6

Text hash 345cdc63dd918bf2027362900dc6d0e925a0bd2b4109f5b439ee0acdcfa592f9; absolute characters 0–2273; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6870-modifying-order-approving-code-fair-competition-for-the-needlework.txt

```text
APPROVED CODE NO. 474—CODE OF FAIR COMPETITION FOR THE NEEDLEWORK INDUSTRY IN PUERTO RICO AS APPROVED ON JUNE 28, 1934

The Executive Order of June 28th approving the Code of Fair Competition for the Needlework Industry in Puerto Rico provided, in part in the fourth condition of approval, as follows:

"That there shall be appointed by the Administrator for Industrial Recovery, within ten days after the effective date hereof, a Puerto Rican Needlework Commission consisting of three persons: one of wdiom shall bo nominated by the Code Authority for the Needlework Industry in Puerto Rico, another of whom shall be nominated by the several Code Authorities of related industries in continental United States, and a third person to serve as chairman, shall be nominated by the National Recovery Administration."

It having been found by the National Industrial Recovery Board that the several Code Authorities of the related industries in the continental United States cannot agree upon a nominee for this Commission, and the said Board having recommended, in its report to me which is approved and attached hereto, a modification of the above-quoted provision.

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin Delano Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do order that the abovequoted provision be and it is hereby revised to read as follows:

"That there shall be established a Puerto Rican Needlework Commission consisting of four members who shall be appointed by the National Industrial Recovery Board as follow's: one member to represent the Needlework Industry in Puerto Rico, on the nomination of the Code Authority for that industry, who shall have one vote; two members to represent the related industries in the continental United States, to be selected by the National Industrial Recovery Board from the nominations made by the Code Authorities for such industries, each of whom shall have one-half vote; one member to serve as impartial chairman, who shall have one vote."

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

Approval Recommended:

     The National Industrial Recovery Board

By G. A. Lynch,

     Administrative Officer

The White House,

Oct 11, 1934.

```

### T4c5078b213a59fc4c85f650a

Text hash 24663521810b58c36731265eba185b03e213c5db6c8ad753edc28b5f3c301bd1; absolute characters 0–2372; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6686-approval-code-fair-competition-for-the-linseed-oil-manufacturing.txt

```text
Whereas, the Secretary of Agriculture and the Administrator of the National Industrial Recovery Act having rendered their separate reports and recommendations and findings on the provisions of said Code, coming within their respective jurisdictions, as set forth in the Executive Order No. 6182 of June 26, 1933, as supplemented by Executive Order No. 6207 of July 21, 1933, Executive Order No. 6345 of October 20, 1933, and Executive Order No. 6551 of January 1934;

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do hereby find that;

1. An application has been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Linseed Oil Manufacturing Industry; and,

2. Due notice and opportunity for hearings to interested parties have been given pursuant to the provisions of the Act and regulations thereunder; and,

3. Hearings have been held upon said Code, pursuant to such notice and pursuant to the pertinent provisions of the Act and regulations thereunder; and,

4. Said Code of Fair Competition constitutes a Code of Fair Competition, as contemplated by the Act and complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of the Act, including clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of Section 3 of Title I of the Act; and

5. It appears, after due consideration, that said Code of Fair Competition will tend to effectuate the policy of Congress as declared in Section 1 of Title I of the Act; and,

6. Three months after the effective date of this Code, the Code Authority shall submit to the Administrator a study of the hours of labor, rates of pay, and other conditions of employment within this Industry, with the view of revising the wages, hours, and other conditions of employment as set forth in this Code.

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do hereby approve said Code of Fair Competition for the Linseed Oil Manufacturing Industry.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

April 20, 1934.

```

### T58bed66d3257a6866672e1b3

Text hash 76a03b7ca273d84ae12ed6031322ee561c42f06799ded3446c0c654a4a8901a1; absolute characters 0–1901; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6601-code-fair-competition-for-the-retail-solid-fuel-industry.txt

```text
An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Retail Solid Fuel Industry, and hearings having been held thereon and the Administrator having rendered liis report containing an analysis of the said Code of Fair Competition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto, and the Administrator having found that the said Code of Fair Competition complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of Title I of said Act and that the requirements of Clauses (1) and (2) of Subsection (a) of Section 3 of the said Act have been met:

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do approve the report and recommendations and adopt the findings of the Administrator and do order that the said Code of Fair Competition be and it is hereby approved, subject to the following condition:

Within 120 days after the effective date of this Code the Administrator shall make a report to the President showing the effect of the Code, and particularly the effect of the provisions of Article V thereof, during the period of 90 days following its effective date, in such detail as to permit the President to determine within said 120 days whether, in order to effectuate the policy of the Act, any of the provisions of the Code should be modified or eliminated. The Code is approved with the specific reservation of the power of the President to make such a determination and to modify this approval in accordance therewith.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

Approval Recommended:

     Hugh S Johnson

          Administrator.

The White House,

February 14 1934.

```

### T6295c02458d556f942a92c17

Text hash 3793b457c7396e1e6604a4b2e9e8f10df7b9de41634686b138c76c050e3e5cda; absolute characters 0–3616; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6871-concerning-amendments-the-code-fair-competition-for-the-cotton.txt

```text
Whereas Executive Order No. 6828, dated August 21, 1934, approved certain amendments to the Code of Fair Competition for the Cotton Garment Industry, including amendments to Articles III and IV thereof, which latter amendments by their terms were not to become effective until October 1, 1934, and

Whereas Executive Order No. 6861, dated September 28, 1934, stayed the effective date of said amendments to Articles III and IV of said Code of Fair Competition to and including October 15, 1934, and directed the National Industrial Recovery Board to appoint a committee of three impartial persons to hear protests, investigate the facts and report its recommendations concerning said amendments on or before October 10, 1934, and

Whereas the Cotton Garment Code Authority and various members of the Cotton Garment Industry, protestants against said amendments, had stated that if such an impartial committee were appointed to determine the issues involved, said protestants would abide by the conclusions of such committee, and

Whereas the National Industrial Recovery Board, pursuant to said Executive Order duly appointed Willard E. Hotchkiss, W. Jett Lauck and Donald M. Nelson, three impartial persons who had not theretofore formed an opinion concerning the subject matter of said amendments, as such committee, which said committee thereafter heard protests, investigated the facts and on October 10, 1934, did report its recommendations in the premises:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act of June 16, 1933 (ch. 90, 48 Stat. 195), and in order to effectuate the purposes of said title and of my said Executive Order No. 6861, dated September 28, 1934, I, FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT, President of the United States, do hereby approve and adopt the report and recommendations of said committee and do order:

1. That paragraph numbered 1 of Executive Order No. 6861, dated September 28, 1934, be and it is hereby revoked.

2. That the effective date of said amendments to Articles III and IV of said Code be stayed to and including December 1, 1934, after which date said amendments shall be in full force and effect.

3. That the committee heretofore appointed and constituted by the National Industrial Recovery Board, consisting of Willard E. Hotchkiss, Chairman, W. Jett Lauck, and Donald M. Nelson, be continued, with instructions further to investigate the protests of the Sheep Lined and Leather Garment Subdivision of the Cotton Garment Industry and report its findings and recommendations therein on or before November 15, 1934.

4. That the National Industrial Recovery Board forthwith appoint a committee of three impartial persons, which committee shall investigate the effects of competition between the products of prison labor and sheltered workshops on the one hand and of the cotton garment industry on the other, study the operation of the Prison Labor Compact especially as to the enforcement of the standards of competition with private industry established therein, and report to the National Industrial Recovery Board concerning said matters not later than December 1, 1934.

5. That on or before January 15, 1935, the National Industrial Recovery Board report to me as to exceptions to and exemptions from the Code of Fair Competition for the Cotton Garment Industry, as amended, which may have been granted under the provisions of Article XV of said Code as amended.

6. That this Order shall be subject to my further orders in the premises.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

October 12, 1934.

```

### T6980966f0258491b7b5ed6be

Text hash c2d286438916b42a1edf91ad8c8131e1fef72fa8a319aab5dceed2e3ac884109; absolute characters 1806–2118; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-app-executive-orders-6686-2c1d3c3b47.txt

```text
Three months after the effective date of this Code, the Code Authority shall submit to the Administrator a study of the hours of labor, rates of pay, and other conditions of employment within this Industry, with the view of revising the wages, hours, and other conditions of employment as set forth in this Code.
```

### T7ebcae684d9ebdc00635faf3

Text hash 186eb0e062eeeea94d2b32274e3d2b83dfa545747d255ace1ad34378bcb992d2; absolute characters 0–1853; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6729-code-fair-competition-for-the-canning-industry.txt

```text
An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Canning Industry, and hearings having been held thereon and the Administrator having rendered his report containing an analysis of said Code of Fair Competition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto, and the Administrator having found that said Code of Fair Competition complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of Title I of said Act and that the requirements of clauses (1) and (a) of subsection (a) of Section 3 of said Act have been met:

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report, recommendations and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said Code of Fair Competition be and it is hereby approved, subject to the following conditions:

(1) That the Industry shall designate a committee whose membership shall be subject to the approval of the Administrator and who shall cooperate with the Administrator in the formulation of standards of quality for products of the Industry and to make recommendations to the Administrator within ninety (90) days for the inclusion in said Code of provisions with respect to such standards and labelling requirements.

(a) That the Industry shall report not later than December 1, 1934 on the operation of the provisions of Articles III and IV with a view to improving the hour and wage provisions of this Code.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

Approval Recommended:

     Hugh S Johnson

          Administrator.

The White House,

May 29, 1934.

```

### T7f93d6e3fd7a52e12a41795d

Text hash f52fb7e349667615f38014be01b4b6e5aa36d7cc16c938b508ddc7ce47b4ea55; absolute characters 0–1748; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6271-code-fair-competition-for-the-wall-paper-manufacturing-industry.txt

```text
An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Wall Paper Manufacturing Industry, and hearings having been held thereon and the Administrator having rendered his report containing an analysis of the said code of fair competition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto, and the Administrator having found that the said code of fair competition complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of title I of said act and that the requirements of clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of section 3 of the said act have been met:

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report, recommendations, and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said code of fair competition be and is hereby approved, subject to the following condition:

(1) To effectuate further the policies of the act, a wall paper manufacturing industry committee be created to cooperate with the Administrator as a Planning and Fair Practice Agency for the Wall Paper Manufacturing Industry, which committee shall consist of five representatives of the wall paper manufacturing industry elected by a fair method of selection, to be approved by the Administrator, and three members without vote appointed by the Administrator.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT,

President.

Approval recommended:

     Hugh S. Johnson.

          Administrator.

The White House,

September 7, 1933.

```

### Ta627ec5afee672ddc410a37d

Text hash 7ecba78f83644ae8f9634fb949c9ea9b1debb993dff4d4b5b06f133a2800b475; absolute characters 0–1670; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6292-code-fair-competition-for-the-oil-burner-industry.txt

```text
An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Oil Burner Industry, and hearings having been held thereon and the Administrator having rendered his report containing an analysis of the said Code of Fair Competition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto, and the Administrator having found that the said Code of Fair Competition complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of title I of said act and that the requirements of clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of section 3 of the said act have been met.

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report, recommendations and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said Code of Fair Competition be and is hereby approved, subject to the following condition:

(1) To effectuate further the policies of the act, a Code Authority be created to cooperate with the Administrator as a Planning and Fair Practice Agency for the Oil Burner Industry, which Code Authority shall consist of nine representatives, of the Oil Burner Industry elected by a fair method of selection, to be approved by the Administrator, and three members without vote appointed by the Administrator.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

Approval recommended:

     Hugh S. Johnson.

          Administrator.

The White House,

September 18, 1933.

```

### Tec1ec659c2eca093a36a2b6e

Text hash 4bc04962998798ec73fd00ee544d797ee14648f5f6988fc01a5604b55c44b4f9; absolute characters 0–1994; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6861-staying-certain-amendments-the-code-fair-competition-for-the-cotton.txt

```text
Whereas, Executive Order No. 6828 of August 21, 1934, approved, among other amendments of the Code of Fair Competition for the Cotton Garment Industry, amendments of Articles III and IV, which amendments by their terms are not to go into effect until October 1, 1934, and;

Whereas, a reorganization of the National Recovery Administration has just been effected and there are pending before the newly appointed National Industrial Recovery Board protests by the Cotton Garment Code Authority and by various members of the industry against said amendments to Articles III and IV, and it appears that said Board should have an adequate opportunity to investigate the facts and make its recommendations thereon, and;

Whereas, said protestants have stated that if an impartial committee were appointed to determine the issues involved in the protest, they would abide by its conclusions;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act of June 16, 1933 (c. 90, 48 Stat. 195), and in order to effectuate the purposes of said title, I hereby order:

1. That said amendments to Articles III and IV of the Code of Fair Competition for the Cotton Garment Industry be stayed up to and including October 15, 1934.

2. That the National Industrial Recovery Board shall forthwith appoint a committee of three impartial persons who have not heretofore formed an opinion concerning the subject matter of said amendments, which committee shall hear said protests, investigate the facts and report its recommendations on or before October 10, 1934.

3. That the National Industrial Recovery Board shall immediately transmit said recommendations to me.

This order is made upon the understanding that the protestants will accept as final and will abide by such decision and order as I shall make on or before October 15, 1934, after consideration of said recommendations.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

September 28, 1934.

```

and identity: EO 6686 is read in full in the registered American Presidency Project (APP) transcription jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6686-approval-code-fair-competition-for-the-linseed-oil-manufacturing.txt (grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-29: the row with the same sha256 (2c1d3c3b47e4...) in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl is a common-crawl capture (, captured 2023-09-07T18:14:56Z, WARC location recorded so the page can be re-fetched) whose heading is 'Executive Order 6686—Approval of Code of Fair Competition for the Linseed Oil Manufacturing Industry' and whose date is 1934-04-20; that date agrees with the date line of the held text ('April 20, 1934.'), and the row's text_sha256 equals the held file's. The held file is the whole order (recital, operative paragraph, signature, the Administrator's recommendation and the date line); it carries no order number, so the number comes from the page record. Clauses and sources are quoted from this registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. Identity: number, date and subject agree across the APP page record, the census register ('Executive Order 6686—Approval of Code of Fair Competition for the Linseed Oil Manufacturing Industry', 1934-04-20) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (printed page 576: 'Code of Fair Competition for the Linseed Oil Manufac turing Industry approved.'). The recital names the report of the Secretary of Agriculture and of the Administrator under EO 6182 as supplemented; the register and WPA titles both name the approval of the Linseed Oil code. EO 6686 (April 20, 1934, Franklin D. Roosevelt) recites that the Secretary of Agriculture and the Administrator reported separately on the provisions within their respective jurisdictions, finds in five numbered paragraphs that the application, notice, hearings and statutory conditions were met, adds in paragraph 6 that 'Three months after the effective date of this Code, the Code Authority shall submit to the Administrator a study of the hours of labor, rates of pay, and other conditions of employment within this Industry', and in a final paragraph approves the Code of Fair Competition for the Linseed Oil Manufacturing Industry. The machine inventory was wrong (six clauses made of the findings; the approval omitted), so the inventory is corrected with two spans: the report command and the approval. Legal reading: both clauses depend on section 3. The approval has no period of its own. The report command is a condition of approval that section 3(a) expressly authorizes ('requirements for the making of reports'), addressed to the Code Authority the code creates; its three-month period is a deadline for a report, which under the owner's 2026-10-21 report-deadline ruling is not an end, and the code's effective date is not held, so the deadline could not be dated in any case. Both clauses end with the repeal on 1935-06-14.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No own-terms end for the approval. The report command's 'three months after the effective date of this Code' is a report deadline; it is not an end (owner ruling 2026-10-21, report deadlines), no held record shows whether or when the study was submitted, and the code's effective date is not held. The command therefore stays on the books until its legal basis is removed on 1935-06-14. The name search found EO 6551 (January 1934), which transfers the Secretary of Agriculture's delegated functions to the Administrator and lists Linseed Oil among the first-processor industries for which certain exercises of those functions need the Secretary's written approval; it is a delegation order that predates this approval and does not cancel or suspend the code. No later held order names the Linseed Oil code. Searches made on 2026-10-29: (1) a full-text search of every held order text numbered 6200 through 7100 under jobs/*/output/texts (APP, HathiTrust GPO, Wikisource and Federal Register texts; clause-drafts copies excluded) for the code's name; (2) a citation search of the same texts for this order's number; (3) a search of the same texts for general NRA orders that could suspend, cancel or supersede codes: the held general orders (EO 6354 as amended by EO 6710, EO 6646, 6711, 6750-C, 6764 and 6829) prescribe rules, contract terms and delegations, and none suspends, cancels or supersedes the code named here; (4) the census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list, read for this code's name; they show no cancellation, suspension or superseding code. Orders for which no text is held (for example EO 6454) could not be searched, so the search covers held texts only. No held record shows this code cancelled or modified under section 10(b), superseded by another code, revoked or suspended before June 14, 1935. June 16, 1935 is not an end: it was Title I's original two-year expiry under section 2(c), which section 1 of Senate Joint Resolution 113 replaced with April 1, 1936. The approved code's own text is not held (it is printed in the NRA Codes volumes the WPA list cites), so a code-internal expiry or effective-date term could not be read; no held order records one for this code. Schechter (295 U.S. 495, May 27, 1935) is recorded in source_notes as a validity characterization, not as an end date: it reversed one conviction and held the Live Poultry Code provisions invalid, it did not cancel or withdraw this approving order, and no held record shows this code cancelled or suspended on that date.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The report command is addressed to the Code Authority and concerns the hours, rates of pay and conditions 'within this Industry' as set forth in this code, with a view to revising them; it is a condition of the approval, not a delegation under another statute and not an appointment. It has its own end (the repeal) and its own deadline note. The order appoints no one and creates no body.\",\"savings_check\":\"Acts done and liabilities incurred under the code while the approval was in force are accrued effects, and the section 5 antitrust exemption for action complying with the approved code taken while Title I was in effect (and for sixty days after) is an accrued effect for that completed action. The proviso to section 2 of Senate Joint Resolution 113 narrows the exemption to agreements and action thereunder that put section 7(a) requirements into effect or prohibit practices already unlawful; it names agreements, states no time limit, and the held text does not settle whether it narrowed the exemption for action under codes before June 14, 1935. No held source shows a prosecution continued after June 14, 1935, and none continues the approval itself. Whether the Code Authority submitted the study is not shown in any held source; a study submitted before the repeal would be an accrued, completed act, and nothing in the order obliges anyone to submit one after the repeal.\",\"scope_check\":\"Corrected inventory: (a) paragraph 6, the report command; (b) the final approval paragraph. Paragraphs 1 to 5 are findings and recitals and are not commands; the recital about the Secretary of Agriculture's and the Administrator's separate reports is not a command either. and identity: EO 6686 is read in full in the registered American Presidency Project (APP) transcription jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6686-approval-code-fair-competition-for-the-linseed-oil-manufacturing.txt (grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-29: the row with the same sha256 (2c1d3c3b47e4...) in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl is a common-crawl capture (, captured 2023-09-07T18:14:56Z, WARC location recorded so the page can be re-fetched) whose heading is 'Executive Order 6686—Approval of Code of Fair Competition for the Linseed Oil Manufacturing Industry' and whose date is 1934-04-20; that date agrees with the date line of the held text ('April 20, 1934.'), and the row's text_sha256 equals the held file's. The held file is the whole order (recital, operative paragraph, signature, the Administrator's recommendation and the date line); it carries no order number, so the number comes from the page record. Clauses and sources are quoted from this registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. Identity: number, date and subject agree across the APP page record, the census register ('Executive Order 6686—Approval of Code of Fair Competition for the Linseed Oil Manufacturing Industry', 1934-04-20) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (printed page 576: 'Code of Fair Competition for the Linseed Oil Manufac turing Industry approved.'). The recital names the report of the Secretary of Agriculture and of the Administrator under EO 6182 as supplemented; the register and WPA titles both name the approval of the Linseed Oil code. EO 6686 (April 20, 1934, Franklin D. Roosevelt) recites that the Secretary of Agriculture and the Administrator reported separately on the provisions within their respective jurisdictions, finds in five numbered paragraphs that the application, notice, hearings and statutory conditions were met, adds in paragraph 6 that 'Three months after the effective date of this Code, the Code Authority shall submit to the Administrator a study of the hours of labor, rates of pay, and other conditions of employment within this Industry', and in a final paragraph approves the Code of Fair Competition for the Linseed Oil Manufacturing Industry. The machine inventory was wrong (six clauses made of the findings; the approval omitted), so the inventory is corrected with two spans: the report command and the approval. Legal reading: both clauses depend on section 3. The approval has no period of its own. The report command is a condition of approval that section 3(a) expressly authorizes ('requirements for the making of reports'), addressed to the Code Authority the code creates; its three-month period is a deadline for a report, which under the owner's 2026-10-21 report-deadline ruling is not an end, and the code's effective date is not held, so the deadline could not be dated in any case. Both clauses end with the repeal on 1935-06-14.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No own-terms end for the approval. The report command's 'three months after the effective date of this Code' is a report deadline; it is not an end (owner ruling 2026-10-21, report deadlines), no held record shows whether or when the study was submitted, and the code's effective date is not held. The command therefore stays on the books until its legal basis is removed on 1935-06-14. The name search found EO 6551 (January 1934), which transfers the Secretary of Agriculture's delegated functions to the Administrator and lists Linseed Oil among the first-processor industries for which certain exercises of those functions need the Secretary's written approval; it is a delegation order that predates this approval and does not cancel or suspend the code. No later held order names the Linseed Oil code. Searches made on 2026-10-29: (1) a full-text search of every held order text numbered 6200 through 7100 under jobs/*/output/texts (APP, HathiTrust GPO, Wikisource and Federal Register texts; clause-drafts copies excluded) for the code's name; (2) a citation search of the same texts for this order's number; (3) a search of the same texts for general NRA orders that could suspend, cancel or supersede codes: the held general orders (EO 6354 as amended by EO 6710, EO 6646, 6711, 6750-C, 6764 and 6829) prescribe rules, contract terms and delegations, and none suspends, cancels or supersedes the code named here; (4) the census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list, read for this code's name; they show no cancellation, suspension or superseding code. Orders for which no text is held (for example EO 6454) could not be searched, so the search covers held texts only. No held record shows this code cancelled or modified under section 10(b), superseded by another code, revoked or suspended before June 14, 1935. June 16, 1935 is not an end: it was Title I's original two-year expiry under section 2(c), which section 1 of Senate Joint Resolution 113 replaced with April 1, 1936. The approved code's own text is not held (it is printed in the NRA Codes volumes the WPA list cites), so a code-internal expiry or effective-date term could not be read; no held order records one for this code. Schechter (295 U.S. 495, May 27, 1935) is recorded in source_notes as a validity characterization, not as an end date: it reversed one conviction and held the Live Poultry Code provisions invalid, it did not cancel or withdraw this approving order, and no held record shows this code cancelled or suspended on that date.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The report command is addressed to the Code Authority and concerns the hours, rates of pay and conditions 'within this Industry' as set forth in this code, with a view to revising them; it is a condition of the approval, not a delegation under another statute and not an appointment. It has its own end (the repeal) and its own deadline note. The order appoints no one and creates no body.\",\"savings_check\":\"Acts done and liabilities incurred under the code while the approval was in force are accrued effects, and the section 5 antitrust exemption for action complying with the approved code taken while Title I was in effect (and for sixty days after) is an accrued effect for that completed action. The proviso to section 2 of Senate Joint Resolution 113 narrows the exemption to agreements and action thereunder that put section 7(a) requirements into effect or prohibit practices already unlawful; it names agreements, states no time limit, and the held text does not settle whether it narrowed the exemption for action under codes before June 14, 1935. No held source shows a prosecution continued after June 14, 1935, and none continues the approval itself. Whether the Code Authority submitted the study is not shown in any held source; a study submitted before the repeal would be an accrued, completed act, and nothing in the order obliges anyone to submit one after the repeal.\",\"scope_check\":\"Corrected inventory: (a) paragraph 6, the report command; (b) the final approval paragraph. Paragraphs 1 to 5 are findings and recitals and are not commands; the recital about the Secretary of Agriculture's and the Administrator's separate reports is not a command either. The signature and the date line command nothing. The order is an approval under Title I; it is not an instrument issued under the Agricultural Adjustment Act, and no part of it delegates anything to the Secretary of Agriculture.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6686#corrected-1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6686#corrected-1"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6686#corrected-2"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6686#corrected-2"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","state":"match"},"sha256":"98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305","text_path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","text_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"24663521810b58c36731265eba185b03e213c5db6c8ad753edc28b5f3c301bd1","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6686-approval-code-fair-competition-for-the-linseed-oil-manufacturing.txt","sha256":"24663521810b58c36731265eba185b03e213c5db6c8ad753edc28b5f3c301bd1","state":"match"},"sha256":"2c1d3c3b47e40bf336249c5b7f455ef0b0b5ea982aa13ffd3bf109be58d39a3f","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6686-approval-code-fair-competition-for-the-linseed-oil-manufacturing.txt","text_sha256":"24663521810b58c36731265eba185b03e213c5db6c8ad753edc28b5f3c301bd1"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"56151e98982e033e42df886d1b2da028765c9958d182864575883e58c79521c9","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/c9fcb1910615c2d9b677700a8fa96edc8da2cfdca204ed04f51e5f8dde99ebe7/6fca72c4672ad8a Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-29: the row with the same sha256 (03fb919b24d6...) in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl is a common-crawl capture (CC-MAIN-2025-43, captured 2025-10-17T18:39:17Z, WARC location recorded so the page can be re-fetched) whose heading is 'Executive Order 6729—Code of Fair Competition for the Canning Industry' and whose date is 1934-05-29; that date agrees with the date line of the held text ('May 29, 1934.'), and the row's text_sha256 equals the held file's. The held file is the whole order (recital, operative paragraph, signature, the Administrator's recommendation and the date line); it carries no order number, so the number comes from the page record. Clauses and sources are quoted from this registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. Identity: number, date and subject agree across the APP page record, the census register ('Executive Order 6729—Code of Fair Competition for the Canning Industry', 1934-05-29) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (printed page 580: 'Code of Fair Competition for the Canning Industry ap proved. XI NRA Codes 25.'). The text has a transcription slip in the recital ('clauses (1) and (a)' for '(1) and (2)') that does not affect the operative words; the WPA list adds the citation XI NRA Codes 25. EO 6729 (May 29, 1934, Franklin D. Roosevelt) adopts the Administrator's report, recommendations and findings and orders the Code of Fair Competition for the Canning Industry approved 'subject to the following conditions': (1) that the Industry designate a committee, subject to the Administrator's approval, to cooperate with the Administrator in formulating standards of quality and labelling requirements and 'to make recommendations to the Administrator within ninety (90) days' for inclusion in the code; and (a) that the Industry 'report not later than December 1, 1934 on the operation of the provisions of Articles III and IV with a view to improving the hour and wage provisions of this Code'. Legal reading: three clauses (the machine inventory is correct): the approval, the committee condition and the report condition. All three depend on section 3(a), which authorizes conditions of approval including 'requirements for the making of reports'. The approval has no period of its own. The conditions' ninety days (with no stated starting point) and December 1, 1934 are deadlines for recommendations and a report; they are not ends (owner ruling 2026-10-21, report deadlines), and no held record shows whether the committee was designated or the report made. Each clause therefore ends with the repeal on 1935-06-14, which removed the approval these conditions qualify.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No own-terms end for the approval. For the committee condition and the report condition the stated periods are deadlines, not ends; the text gives no starting point for the ninety days, and no held record shows performance, so no earlier end is established for either. The name search found only this order within the range; the Canning and Packing Machinery Industry code (EO 6378 of October 31, 1933) is a different code. Searches made on 2026-10-29: (1) a full-text search of every held order text numbered 6200 through 7100 under jobs/*/output/texts (APP, HathiTrust GPO, Wikisource and Federal Register texts; clause-drafts copies excluded) for the code's name; (2) a citation search of the same texts for this order's number; (3) a search of the same texts for general NRA orders that could suspend, cancel or supersede codes: the held general orders (EO 6354 as amended by EO 6710, EO 6646, 6711, 6750-C, 6764 and 6829) prescribe rules, contract terms and delegations, and none suspends, cancels or supersedes the code named here; (4) the census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list, read for this code's name; they show no cancellation, suspension or superseding code. Orders for which no text is held (for example EO 6454) could not be searched, so the search covers held texts only. No held record shows this code cancelled or modified under section 10(b), superseded by another code, revoked or suspended before June 14, 1935. June 16, 1935 is not an end: it was Title I's original two-year expiry under section 2(c), which section 1 of Senate Joint Resolution 113 replaced with April 1, 1936. The approved code's own text is not held (it is printed in the NRA Codes volumes the WPA list cites), so a code-internal expiry or effective-date term could not be read; no held order records one for this code. Schechter (295 U.S. 495, May 27, 1935) is recorded in source_notes as a validity characterization, not as an end date: it reversed one conviction and held the Live Poultry Code provisions invalid, it did not cancel or withdraw this approving order, and no held record shows this code cancelled or suspended on that date.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Items (1) and (a) are conditions of the approval addressed to 'the Industry', to cooperate with the Administrator on standards for inclusion in this code and to report on the operation of this code's Articles III and IV; they are not appointments by the President, delegations under another statute or directions to an agency. The committee's membership 'shall be subject to the approval of the Administrator' but the order appoints no one. No held source shows a prosecution continued after June 14, 1935, and none continues the approval itself. standing note: original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-29: the row with the same sha256 (3243c055d6bd...) in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl is a common-crawl capture (CC-MAIN-2025-43, captured 2025-10-08T21:39:31Z, WARC location recorded so the page can be re-fetched) whose heading is 'Executive Order 6870-A—Modifying Order Approving Code of Fair Competition for the Needlework Industry in Puerto Rico to Alter the Constitution of the Puerto Rican Needlework Commission' and whose date is 1934-10-11; that date agrees with the date line of the held text ('Oct 11, 1934.'), and the row's text_sha256 equals the held file's. The held file is the whole order (recital, operative paragraph, signature, the Administrator's recommendation and the date line); it carries no order number, so the number comes from the page record. Clauses and sources are quoted from this registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. Identity: number, date and subject agree across the APP page record, the census register ('Executive Order 6870-A—Modifying Order Approving Code of Fair Competition for the Needlework Industry in Puerto Rico to Alter the Constitution of the Puerto Rican Needlework Commission', 1934-10-11) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (printed page 593: 'Amendments to the Code of Fair Competition for the Needle Industry in Puerto Rico approved.'). The WPA list calls the order 'Amendments to the Code of Fair Competition for the Needle Industry in Puerto Rico approved'; the held text instead revises the fourth condition of the June 28, 1934 approval of the Needlework Industry code for Puerto Rico (the approving order is held as EO 6756-B), so the WPA summary is a looser description of the same order, with number, date and industry agreeing. EO 6870-A (October 11, 1934, Franklin D. Roosevelt) recites the fourth condition of the June 28, 1934 approval of the Code of Fair Competition for the Needlework Industry in Puerto Rico (that the Administrator appoint, within ten days of the effective date, a three-person Puerto Rican Needlework Commission with members nominated by the Puerto Rican Code Authority, the continental related-industry Code Authorities and the National Recovery Administration), recites that the continental Code Authorities cannot agree upon a nominee and the Board recommended a modification, and orders that the quoted provision 'be and it is hereby revised to read as follows: ''That there shall be established a Puerto Rican Needlework Commission consisting of four members who shall be appointed by the National Industrial Recovery Board''' (one member for the Puerto Rican industry with one vote, two for the continental related industries with one-half vote each, and an impartial chairman with one vote). The machine inventory was a single clause starting inside the quotation of the old condition, so the inventory is corrected to two clauses: the revising order and the replacement condition with its appointment direction. Legal reading: both clauses modify a condition of a section 3(a) approval, which section 3(a) authorizes ('such conditions'). The Commission's only function, set out in the approving order, is to study the operation of the code against competing codes and recommend modifications of the code to the Administrator, and to recommend on piece rates; so the direction to appoint it has no operation independent of the code. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 strike-and-insert ruling the amended condition continues, amended, until the parent approval ends. No held record shows whether or when the Board appointed the four members; that is not needed for the end, because the Commission and the Board's duty to appoint it exist as a condition of the approval and have no object once the approval and enforcement provisions are repealed. If appointments were made earlier, that performance would not have ended the Commission's powers, which continued as part of the condition until the repeal on 1935-06-14.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No own-terms end: the revised condition states no period or date (the machine's fixed-term records in the old quotation are the ten-day appointment period of the old condition, not an end of this order). The name search found the approving order EO 6756-B (June 28, 1934) and nothing after this order naming the Needlework Industry code of Puerto Rico; no held order cancels, supersedes or modifies it again, and no held order abolishes or replaces the Commission before June 14, 1935. Searches made on 2026-10-29: (1) a full-text search of every held order text numbered 6200 through 7100 under jobs/*/output/texts (APP, HathiTrust GPO, Wikisource and Federal Register texts; clause-drafts copies excluded) for the code's name; (2) a citation search of the same texts for this order's number; (3) a search of the same texts for general NRA orders that could suspend, cancel or supersede codes: the held general orders (EO 6354 as amended by EO 6710, EO 6646, 6711, 6750-C, 6764 and 6829) prescribe rules, contract terms and delegations, and none suspends, cancels or supersedes the code named here; (4) the census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list, read for this code's name; they show no cancellation, suspension or superseding code. Orders for which no text is held (for example EO 6454) could not be searched, so the search covers held texts only. No held record shows this code cancelled or modified under section 10(b), superseded by another code, revoked or suspended before June 14, 1935. June 16, 1935 is not an end: it was Title I's original two-year expiry under section 2(c), which section 1 of Senate Joint Resolution 113 replaced with April 1, 1936. The approved code's own text is not held (it is printed in the NRA Codes volumes the WPA list cites), so a code-internal expiry or effective-date term could not be read; no held order records one for this code. Schechter (295 U.S. 495, May 27, 1935) is recorded in source_notes as a validity characterization, not as an end date: it reversed one conviction and held the Live Poultry Code provisions invalid, it did not cancel or withdraw this approving order, and no held record shows this code cancelled or suspended on that date.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The direction that the Board appoint the Commission's four members is the only command that could be called independent of the code. It is not: it is part of the replacement of a condition of the code's approval, its object is a body whose sole functions are to study and recommend on the operation of this code, and nothing in the order creates a function outside the code. It nevertheless has its own clause and its own end reasoning. The order delegates nothing under another statute.\",\"savings_check\":\"Acts done and liabilities incurred under the code while the approval was in force are accrued effects, and the section 5 antitrust exemption for action complying with the approved code taken while Title I was in effect (and for sixty days after) is an accrued effect for that completed action. The proviso to section 2 of Senate Joint Resolution 113 narrows the exemption to agreements and action thereunder that put section 7(a) requirements into effect or prohibit practices already unlawful; it names agreements, states no time limit, and the held text does not settle whether it narrowed the exemption for action under codes before June 14, 1935. No held source shows a prosecution continued after June 14, 1935, and none continues the approval itself. Members appointed to the Commission before the repeal, and any recommendations the Commission made, are completed acts; no held source shows either, and nothing obliges the Board to appoint after the repeal.\",\"scope_check\":\"Corrected inventory: (a) the operative revising order; (b) the replacement condition. The recital of the old condition (the quoted ten-day, three-person text), the recital of the Board's finding and report, the Administrator-style recommendation block ('Approval Recommended: The National Industrial Recovery Board By G. A. jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6870-modifying-order-approving-code-fair-competition-for-the-needlework.txt (grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-29: the row with the same sha256 (3243c055d6bd...) in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl is a common-crawl capture (CC-MAIN-2025-43, captured 2025-10-08T21:39:31Z, WARC location recorded so the page can be re-fetched) whose heading is 'Executive Order 6870-A—Modifying Order Approving Code of Fair Competition for the Needlework Industry in Puerto Rico to Alter the Constitution of the Puerto Rican Needlework Commission' and whose date is 1934-10-11; that date agrees with the date line of the held text ('Oct 11, 1934.'), and the row's text_sha256 equals the held file's. The held file is the whole order (recital, operative paragraph, signature, the Administrator's recommendation and the date line); it carries no order number, so the number comes from the page record. Clauses and sources are quoted from this registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. Identity: number, date and subject agree across the APP page record, the census register ('Executive Order 6870-A—Modifying Order Approving Code of Fair Competition for the Needlework Industry in Puerto Rico to Alter the Constitution of the Puerto Rican Needlework Commission', 1934-10-11) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (printed page 593: 'Amendments to the Code of Fair Competition for the Needle Industry in Puerto Rico approved.'). The WPA list calls the order 'Amendments to the Code of Fair Competition for the Needle Industry in Puerto Rico approved'; the held text instead revises the fourth condition of the June 28, 1934 approval of the Needlework Industry code for Puerto Rico (the approving order is held as EO 6756-B), so the WPA summary is a looser description of the same order, with number, date and industry agreeing. EO 6870-A (October 11, 1934, Franklin D. Roosevelt) recites the fourth condition of the June 28, 1934 approval of the Code of Fair Competition for the Needlework Industry in Puerto Rico (that the Administrator appoint, within ten days of the effective date, a three-person Puerto Rican Needlework Commission with members nominated by the Puerto Rican Code Authority, the continental related-industry Code Authorities and the National Recovery Administration), recites that the continental Code Authorities cannot agree upon a nominee and the Board recommended a modification, and orders that the quoted provision 'be and it is hereby revised to read as follows: ''That there shall be established a Puerto Rican Needlework Commission consisting of four members who shall be appointed by the National Industrial Recovery Board''' (one member for the Puerto Rican industry with one vote, two for the continental related industries with one-half vote each, and an impartial chairman with one vote). The machine inventory was a single clause starting inside the quotation of the old condition, so the inventory is corrected to two clauses: the revising order and the replacement condition with its appointment direction. Legal reading: both clauses modify a condition of a section 3(a) approval, which section 3(a) authorizes ('such conditions'). The Commission's only function, set out in the approving order, is to study the operation of the code against competing codes and recommend modifications of the code to the Administrator, and to recommend on piece rates; so the direction to appoint it has no operation independent of the code. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 strike-and-insert ruling the amended condition continues, amended, until the parent approval ends. No held record shows whether or when the Board appointed the four members; that is not needed for the end, because the Commission and the Board's duty to appoint it exist as a condition of the approval and have no object once the approval and enforcement provisions are repealed. If appointments were made earlier, that performance would not have ended the Commission's powers, which continued as part of the condition until the repeal on 1935-06-14.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No own-terms end: the revised condition states no period or date (the machine's fixed-term records in the old quotation are the ten-day appointment period of the old condition, not an end of this order). The name search found the approving order EO 6756-B (June 28, 1934) and nothing after this order naming the Needlework Industry code of Puerto Rico; no held order cancels, supersedes or modifies it again, and no held order abolishes or replaces the Commission before June 14, 1935. Searches made on 2026-10-29: (1) a full-text search of every held order text numbered 6200 through 7100 under jobs/*/output/texts (APP, HathiTrust GPO, Wikisource and Federal Register texts; clause-drafts copies excluded) for the code's name; (2) a citation search of the same texts for this order's number; (3) a search of the same texts for general NRA orders that could suspend, cancel or supersede codes: the held general orders (EO 6354 as amended by EO 6710, EO 6646, 6711, 6750-C, 6764 and 6829) prescribe rules, contract terms and delegations, and none suspends, cancels or supersedes the code named here; (4) the census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list, read for this code's name; they show no cancellation, suspension or superseding code. Orders for which no text is held (for example EO 6454) could not be searched, so the search covers held texts only. No held record shows this code cancelled or modified under section 10(b), superseded by another code, revoked or suspended before June 14, 1935. June 16, 1935 is not an end: it was Title I's original two-year expiry under section 2(c), which section 1 of Senate Joint Resolution 113 replaced with April 1, 1936. The approved code's own text is not held (it is printed in the NRA Codes volumes the WPA list cites), so a code-internal expiry or effective-date term could not be read; no held order records one for this code. Schechter (295 U.S. 495, May 27, 1935) is recorded in source_notes as a validity characterization, not as an end date: it reversed one conviction and held the Live Poultry Code provisions invalid, it did not cancel or withdraw this approving order, and no held record shows this code cancelled or suspended on that date.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The direction that the Board appoint the Commission's four members is the only command that could be called independent of the code. It is not: it is part of the replacement of a condition of the code's approval, its object is a body whose sole functions are to study and recommend on the operation of this code, and nothing in the order creates a function outside the code. It nevertheless has its own clause and its own end reasoning. The order delegates nothing under another statute.\",\"savings_check\":\"Acts done and liabilities incurred under the code while the approval was in force are accrued effects, and the section 5 antitrust exemption for action complying with the approved code taken while Title I was in effect (and for sixty days after) is an accrued effect for that completed action. The proviso to section 2 of Senate Joint Resolution 113 narrows the exemption to agreements and action thereunder that put section 7(a) requirements into effect or prohibit practices already unlawful; it names agreements, states no time limit, and the held text does not settle whether it narrowed the exemption for action under codes before June 14, 1935. No held source shows a prosecution continued after June 14, 1935, and none continues the approval itself. Members appointed to the Commission before the repeal, and any recommendations the Commission made, are completed acts; no held source shows either, and nothing obliges the Board to appoint after the repeal.\",\"scope_check\":\"Corrected inventory: (a) the operative revising order; (b) the replacement condition. The recital of the old condition (the quoted ten-day, three-person text), the recital of the Board's finding and report, the Administrator-style recommendation block ('Approval Recommended: The National Industrial Recovery Board By G. A. Lynch') and the date line command nothing; the report the order approves 'attached hereto' is not part of the held text.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"6172cf8b3dc863d924d09cc26801b6f062dcd90877faffbd479d89f4d2ef341a","path":"research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t2/packet/supp/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t2/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:4","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"6172cf8b3dc863d924d09cc26801b6f062dcd90877faffbd479d89f4d2ef341a","path":"research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t2/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:4","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6870A#corrected-1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6870A#corrected-1"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6870A#corrected-2"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6870A#corrected-2"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","state":"match"},"sha256":"98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305","text_path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","text_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"345cdc63dd918bf2027362900dc6d0e925a0bd2b4109f5b439ee0acdcfa592f9","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive - **ACT:EO:6861>EO:6871@c15ec3edbb01d5f0**; Se98a83335c3b361ae6b87c4a; {"actor_id":"EO:6871","char_end":2173,"char_start":2064,"confidence":"medium","effective_class":"unknown","effective_date":null,"effective_wording":[{"char_end":459,"char_start":162,"text":"Whereas Executive Order No. 6828, dated August 21, 1934, approved certain amendments to the Code of Fair Competition for the Cotton Garment Industry, including amendments to Articles III and IV thereof, which latter amendments by their terms were not to become effective until October 1, 1934, and"},{"char_end":891,"char_start":461,"text":"Whereas Executive Order No. 6861, dated September 28, 1934, stayed the effective date of said amendments to Articles III and IV of said Code of Fair Competition to and including October 15, 1934, and directed the National Industrial Recovery Board to appoint a committee of three impartial persons to hear protests, investigate the facts and report its recommendations concerning said amendments on or before October 10, 1934, and"},{"char_end":2369,"char_start":2178,"text":"That the effective date of said amendments to Articles III and IV of said Code be stayed to and including December 1, 1934, after which date said amendments shall be in full force and effect."}],"exceptions_text":[],"filing_date":null,"id":"ACT:EO:6861>EO:6871@c15ec3edbb01d5f0","names_target":true,"notes":"Machine lead only; no legal ending asserted. Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-29: the row with the same sha256 (1369a32ed467...) in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl is a common-crawl capture (CC-MAIN-2024-42, captured 2024-10-15T03:46:11Z, WARC location recorded so the page can be re-fetched) whose heading is 'Executive Order 6861—Staying Certain Amendments to the Code of Fair Competition for the Cotton Garment Industry Approved by Executive Order No. 6828 of August 21, 1934' and whose date is 1934-09-28; that date agrees with the date line of the held text ('September 28, 1934.'), and the row's text_sha256 equals the held file's. The held file is the whole order (recital, operative paragraph, signature, the Administrator's recommendation and the date line); it carries no order number, so the number comes from the page record. Clauses and sources are quoted from this registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. Identity: number, date and subject agree across the APP page record, the census register ('Executive Order 6861—Staying Certain Amendments to the Code of Fair Competition for the Cotton Garment Industry Approved by Executive Order No. 6828 of August 21, 1934', 1934-09-28) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (printed page 592: 'Effectivedate of amendments to ArticlesIII and IV of the Code of Fair Competition for the Cotton Garment Industry stayed untilOct. 15, 1934, and the National IndustrialRecovery Board ordered to appoint a committee of three to hear protestsagainst said amendments. XVII NRA Codes 523.'). The WPA list says the order stays the effective date of amendments to Articles III and IV of the Cotton Garment Code until October 15, 1934 and orders the Board to appoint a committee of three (XVII NRA Codes 523). The later order EO 6871 (the revoking order) is also page-checked: its APP page record (node 362578, common-crawl capture 2025-03-23) has the heading 'Executive Order 6871', date 1934-10-12 as in the text's date line, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. EO 6861 (September 28, 1934, Franklin D. Roosevelt), made 'in order to effectuate the purposes of said title', orders: (1) that the amendments to Articles III and IV of the Cotton Garment Code approved by EO 6828 'be stayed up to and including October 15, 1934'; (2) that the National Industrial Recovery Board 'shall forthwith appoint a committee of three impartial persons' who will 'hear said protests, investigate the facts and report its recommendations on or before October 10, 1934'; (3) that the Board 'shall immediately transmit said recommendations to me'. This is a mixed order, so each command has its own end. The machine inventory (a lead plus three items) is corrected to the three commands. Clause 1 (the stay): EO 6871 (October 12, 1934), paragraph 1, orders 'That paragraph numbered 1 of Executive Order No. 6861 ... be and it is hereby revoked.' The first end is that revocation on 1934-10-12, three days before the stay's own stated end (October 15, inclusive; exclusive reading: when October 15 began). EO 6871 paragraph 2 stays the same amendments to and including December 1, 1934; that is a separate new stay by the later order, recorded as a later event, not an extension of this stay (the stay and the revocation are express, so the strike-and-insert and further-exemption rulings do not turn the revocation into an amendment). Clause 2 (committee): EO 6871 recites that the Board 'pursuant to said Executive Order duly appointed Willard E. Hotchkiss, W. Jett Lauck and Donald M. Nelson ... as such committee, which said committee thereafter heard protests, investigated the facts and on October 10, 1934, did report its recommendations'. The command (appoint, hear, report by October 10) was performed and is exhausted on 1934-10-10, the date of the report; the appointment fell between September 28 and October 10. EO 6871 paragraph 3 continues the committee with new instructions on its own authority, which is a later event of the committee, not a continuation of this command. Clause 3 (transmittal): EO 6871 states that the President 'do[es] hereby approve and adopt the report and recommendations of said committee' on October 12, 1934, so the recommendations had reached him by then; the report was made on October 10. The transmittal was therefore made between October 10 and October 12, 1934. The exact day is not stated; the clause is dated 1934-10-12, the day by which the evidence shows performance, and the interval is stated. The order does not state that the Board itself carried the document, only that the recommendations had reached the President; that is the object of the command.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Clause 1: first end by express revocation (EO 6871 paragraph 1) on 1934-10-12; the own-terms date October 15 would have been later. Clauses 2 and 3: no own-terms end other than their own deadlines (October 10, 1934 for the report), which are report deadlines; the ends are the performance shown in EO 6871's recitals. The name search found EO 6828 (the approval whose amendments are stayed), EO 6871 (the revocation and re-stay) and nothing else naming this order; no other held order revokes, shortens or extends it. Searches made on 2026-10-29: (1) a full-text search of every held order text numbered 6200 through 7100 under jobs/*/output/texts (APP, HathiTrust GPO, Wikisource and Federal Register texts; clause-drafts copies excluded) for the code's name; (2) a citation search of the same texts for this order's number; (3) a search of the same texts for general NRA orders that could suspend, cancel or supersede codes: the held general orders (EO 6354 as amended by EO 6710, EO 6646, 6711, 6750-C, 6764 and 6829) prescribe rules, contract terms and delegations, and none suspends, cancels or supersedes the code named here; (4) the census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list, read for this code's name; they show no cancellation, suspension or superseding code. Orders for which no text is held (for example EO 6454) could not be searched, so the search covers held texts only. No held record shows the Cotton Garment code cancelled before June 14, 1935; none was needed, because every clause ended in October 1934. Schechter (May 27, 1935) and the June 14, 1935 repeal come after every clause's end and are not ends of this order.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Paragraphs 2 and 3 are directions to a Title I agency (the National Industrial Recovery Board) and are independent of the stay's continued operation; each therefore has its own end (the report on October 10, 1934 and the transmittal by October 12, 1934), shown by the recitals of EO 6871 and not assumed from the repeal. Paragraph 1 is the only command that depends on a code approval, and its end is the revocation of October 12, 1934.\",\"savings_check\":\"The stay's effect, that the amendments to Articles III and IV did not operate between their original date and October 12, 1934 and then under EO 6871's stay until December 1, 1934, is an accrued effect; the committee's report of October 10, 1934 and the President's adoption of it on October 12, 1934 are completed acts. Nothing continues any of the three commands: EO 6871 re-stayed and continued the committee by its own commands.\",\"scope_check\":\"Corrected inventory: paragraphs 1, 2 and 3 as separate commands. The authority recital ('Now, Therefore, by virtue of ... I hereby order:') is dropped because it commands nothing by itself; the three Whereas recitals and the closing 'understanding' sentence are not commands. The stay in paragraph 1 reaches only the amendments to Articles III and IV approved by EO 6828, not the whole approval.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6861#corrected-1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6861#corrected-1"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6861#corrected-2"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6861#corrected-2"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6861#corrected-3"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6861#corrected-3"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"3793b457c7396e1e6604a4b2e9e8f10df7b9de41634686b138c76c050e3e5cda","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6871-concerning-amendments-the-code-fair-competition-for-the-cotton.txt","sha256":"3793b457c7396e1e6604a4b2e9e8f10df7b9de41634686b138c76c050e3e5cda","state":"match"},"sha256":"2ce13ceca0de91bc548fc4f35971a66fd4db27660ec9c5b33afa56320a322c37","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6871-concerning-amendments-the-code-fair-competition-for-the-cotton.txt","text_sha256":"3793b457c7396e1e6604a4b2e9e8f10df7b9de41634686b138c76c050e3e5cda"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"4bc04962998798ec73fd00ee544d797ee14648f5f6988fc01a5604b55c44b4f9","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6861-staying-certain-amendments-the-code-fair-competition-for-the-cotton.txt","sha256":"4bc04962998798ec73fd00ee544d797ee14648f5f6988fc01a5604b55c44b4f9","state":"match"},"sha256":"1369a32ed467c41d5574a39ad15090068da105893270833afc215fb288fe0294","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6861-staying-certain-amendments-the-code-fair-competition-for-the-cotton.txt","text_sha256":"4bc04962998798ec73fd00ee544d797ee14648f5f6988fc01a5604b55c44b4f9"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"0aab4cafdf5cc590869ce95e0b9027542fc6577e72a62a0ecd7d502c29f66e37","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/881859243da21bea9a6026dd5895230e0d8c3c4416afdbe41e50 ## Valla review leads and component attestations

Review lead only. Every case needs separate applicability, earlier-ending, scope, independent-command, savings and independent whole-case review.
Preparation attestations never approve legal conclusions or replace whole-case review.
